⏺ User approved Claude's plan
  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/inherited-pondering-ripple.md · /plan to 
     edit
     Plan: Zone-based quarantine (per-arm) with compliance, delay, and 
     government trigger

     Context

     Replace the current simplistic quarantine (quarantineOnInfectionPct =
     "freeze infected
     in place") with a richer, more realistic model: a quarantine zone — a
     boxed-off corner
     of the map where infected people are relocated and isolated. Quarantine is
     gated by a
     per-arm checkbox (so one arm can run with quarantine, the other without — a
     direct A/B),
     and driven by three knobs split between personal responsibility and
     government policy. This
     lets the user study how compliance, response delay, and the government's
     activation
     threshold change the infection peak and total removed.

     Confirmed decisions

     - Replace the old quarantineOnInfectionPct (freeze-in-place) entirely.
     - Per-arm enable: a Quarantine checkbox lives in each arm's panel; the
     three knobs
     appear only when that arm's checkbox is on.
     - Three knobs:
       - Personal control: Quarantine compliance % (chance a person
     self-isolates when
     infected) and Quarantine delay (sim-seconds after infection before they
     move to the
     zone; temporal).
       - Public policy: Activation threshold % (quarantine only kicks in once
     this % of the
     population has ever been infected — I+R).
     - Zone: off-limits corner box. Compliant infected (once the government
     threshold is met
     and their delay elapses) are moved into the zone and confined there;
     everyone else is kept
     out so they can't wander in and get infected. On recovery a person leaves
     the zone and
     rejoins normal movement.

     Design

     Schema (src/params.js)

     - Remove quarantineOnInfectionPct.
     - Add per-arm params:
       - quarantineEnabled — group "Public policy", new control type
     type:'toggle'
     (boolean), default false, label "Enable quarantine". Always visible.
       - quarantineCompliancePct — "Quarantine compliance", Personal control, %,
     requiresQuarantine:true, default 70.
       - quarantineDelay — "Quarantine delay", Personal control, sim-sec,
     temporal:true,
     requiresQuarantine:true, min 0 max 10 step 0.5, default 2.
       - quarantineTriggerPct — "Activation threshold", Public policy, %,
     requiresQuarantine:true, min 0 max 50 step 1, default 5, help "% of
     population ever
     infected before government quarantine kicks in".
     - paramDefaults() already picks up all keys (incl. the boolean).

     UI (src/ui.js)

     - buildPanel: render type:'toggle' params as a checkbox row (label +
     checkbox,
     no slider). On change, fire onChange(key).
     - Conditional visibility: in addition to requiresHouseholds (gated by
     opts.householdMode),
     gate requiresQuarantine params on paramsObj.quarantineEnabled (read from
     the arm's
     own params). So a param shows only when its arm has quarantine on.
     - applyDiff + fmt: handle boolean values (e.g. show "on"/"off") so a
     differing
     quarantineEnabled/knob highlights correctly between arms.

     App wiring (src/app.js)

     - onParamChange(name, key):
       - quarantineEnabled → rebuild that arm's panel (show/hide the 3 knobs) +
     reset that
     arm for a clean comparison; then updateDiff().
       - quarantineCompliancePct (and existing socialDistancingPct) →
     sim.refreshBehavior().
       - quarantineDelay / quarantineTriggerPct read live — no extra work.
     - balanceArms: after copying params, use rebuildPanels() (not just
     refresh()) so the
     test panel reflects a copied quarantineEnabled (show/hide knobs).
     - No new global control (enable is per-arm, lives in the panel).

     Agent (src/agent.js)

     - Replace isQuarantined with inQuarantine (in the zone). Keep
     _quarantineRoll.

     Engine (src/simulation.js)

     - reset(): this.govQuarantineActive = false; fresh agents have
     inQuarantine=false.
     - _applyBehaviorFlags(): a.willQuarantine = a._quarantineRoll < 
     compliancePct/100. If
     !quarantineEnabled, clear inQuarantine for all (they leave the zone).
     - quarantineZone() helper → rect in a corner from this.width/height
     (e.g. bottom-right, ~32%×32%, small margin). Recomputed from current size.
     - _integrate:
       - If quarantineEnabled: impacted = (I+R)/total; latch
     govQuarantineActive ||= impacted >= quarantineTriggerPct/100.
       - For each infected a: if quarantineEnabled && govQuarantineActive && 
     a.willQuarantine && !a.inQuarantine && a.infectedTimer >= quarantineDelay →
     set inQuarantine=true and
     teleport into a random spot inside the zone (RNG).
       - Movement: inQuarantine agents wander confined to the zone (reflect off
     zone
     edges); everyone else is kept out of the zone expanded by a buffer (via a
     keepOut
     push). In household mode, keep anchors out of the zone (members follow,
     stay out).
       - Recovery (I→R): a.inQuarantine = false (in household mode they resume
     at their home
     slot; in individuals mode they wander out of the zone).
     - Infection loop: skip infected agents that are inQuarantine as contact 
     sources — a
     guaranteed-isolation model (no transmission from quarantined), independent
     of geometry.
     - render:
       - Draw the quarantine zone (only when enabled): tinted fill + dashed
     border + "Quarantine"
     label, under the agents.
       - Household connecting lines: pick the hub from the first non-quarantined
     member and
     skip lines to inQuarantine members. Drop the old yellow "frozen" ring.

     Critical files

     - src/params.js — swap params + new toggle type + requiresQuarantine flag.
     - src/ui.js — checkbox control + quarantine-gated visibility + boolean
     diff/fmt.
     - src/app.js — quarantine change routing, panel rebuild on toggle, balance
     fix.
     - src/agent.js — inQuarantine field.
     - src/simulation.js — zone geometry, activation latch, move-to-zone,
     confinement/keep-out,
     isolation in the infection loop, zone rendering, line handling.

     Verification

     - Syntax: parse-only check of src/*.js via osascript -l JavaScript.
     - Headless engine test (individuals mode, pop 200, initialInfected 7):
       - quarantine off → full spread baseline (removed ≈ 200).
       - quarantine on, compliance 100%, threshold 0%, delay 0 → strongly
     contained (few
     removed) since compliant infected isolate immediately.
       - threshold 20% → quarantine activates only after 20% impacted →
     intermediate outcome,
     higher than threshold 0%.
       - Assert quarantined agents' positions lie inside the zone and
     non-quarantined lie
     outside it; assert willQuarantine tracks compliance %; same-seed
     determinism holds.
     - Manual/browser: open index.html; per arm, tick Enable quarantine → the 3
     knobs
     appear; run: once the impacted threshold is crossed, compliant infected get
     pulled into
     the corner zone and the curve bends; the zone is off-limits to everyone
     else.
     (No browser here — no Node — so this step is for the user.)

     Out of scope

     - Zone capacity limits / hospital overflow (unbounded zone for now).
     - Smooth travel to the zone (teleport-in; recovery teleports back home in
     household mode).

      279 +      var minX = zx - margin, minY = zy - margin;             
      280 +      if (ag.x <= minX || ag.y <= minY) { return; }

          +ath.abs(ag.vx); }

      291 +      if (ag.x < zx) { ag.x = zx; ag.vx = Math.abs(ag.vx); }

          +.abs(ag.vx); }

✻ Crunched for 14m 47s

※ recap: You're building a browser-based SIR epidemic simulator with A/B arms. I just finished the per-arm quarantine-zone feature (verified working) on top of household and social-distancing models. Next action: commit and push the recent changes to GitHub.

❯ What is the behavior of the activation threshold for quarantine? When the     
  threshold kicks in, do people with existing infections also move to the       
  quarantine or not?                                                            

⏺ Yes — when the threshold activates, existing infections also get moved to 
  quarantine, not just people infected afterward.

  Here's the exact behavior in the engine (_integrate in src/simulation.js):

  - Activation (latching): each step it computes impacted = (Infected + Removed)
  / total — i.e. everyone who's ever been infected. Once that reaches
  quarantineTriggerPct, govQuarantineActive flips to true and stays true for the
  rest of the run.
  - Who moves, and when: every step, for every currently-infected agent,
  maybeEnterQuarantine runs and moves them to the zone if all of these hold:
    - quarantine is enabled,
    - govQuarantineActive is true,
    - they comply (willQuarantine, per the compliance %),
    - they've been infected for at least their quarantine delay (infectedTimer 
  >= quarantineDelay),
    - they're not already in the zone.

  This check isn't limited to newly infected people — it's applied to the whole
  infected population each step. So the moment the threshold trips:

  - Anyone already infected longer than the delay (which is typically everyone
  infected before the threshold, since they've been sick a while) is pulled into
  the zone immediately.
  - Anyone infected more recently waits out the remainder of their own delay
  (the delay is measured from each person's own infection time), then goes.
  - Non-compliant infected (outside the compliance %) never go, regardless.

  So the threshold is a one-time "policy switches on" gate: before it, no one
  quarantines; after it, the standing rule applies to the entire infected
  population going forward — including the backlog of existing cases.

  If you'd prefer different semantics — e.g. the policy only applies to people
  infected after activation (existing cases are "missed") — that's a small
  change; let me know.

  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/inherited-pondering-ripple.md · /plan to 
     edit
     Plan: "Communities" population mode (3×3 grid + inter-community travel)

     Context

     Add a third population mode, Communities: the map is a 3×3 grid; 8 cells 
     are
     communities and the bottom-right cell is reserved for the quarantine ward.
     Each person
     lives in one community and is confined to its cell; with a tunable
     probability they take a
     trip to a random other community, mix there for a tunable duration, then
     return home.
     This models geographically separated populations where disease only crosses
     between
     communities via travel — letting the user study travel restrictions as an
     intervention.

     Confirmed decisions

     - Visit & return travel: home community → trip to a random other community
     → linger →
     return home.
     - Always exactly 8 communities (row-major over the 3×3 grid, skipping
     bottom-right).
     The bottom-right cell is always reserved: quarantine ward when quarantine
     is on, empty
     otherwise. Toggling quarantine never rearranges communities.
     - Two per-arm knobs:
       - Public policy: Inter-community travel (chance per sim-second a home
     person starts a
     trip). requiresCommunities.
       - Personal control: Trip duration (sim-seconds spent in the visited
     community before
     returning; temporal). requiresCommunities.
     - Communities is a distinct populationMode (not combined with households).
     - Travel is a teleport between cells (cells are walled; walking across
     would pass
     through other communities). Trips never target the reserved quarantine
     cell.

     Design

     Grid geometry (shared helpers in src/simulation.js)

     - COMMUNITY_CELLS = [[0,0],[1,0],[2,0],[0,1],[1,1],[2,1],[0,2],[1,2]]
     (index 0..7);
     quarantine cell = [2,2].
     - cellRect(col,row) from this.width/height: cw=w/3, ch=h/3, inset by a
     small gap g
     (~5px) so borders are visible. Computed live (robust to resize).
     - communityCellRect(idx) → cellRect(COMMUNITY_CELLS[idx]).
     - quarantineZone() becomes cellRect(2,2) in all modes (unifies the ward
     with the
     grid's bottom-right cell; negligible change to individuals/households).

     Schema (src/params.js)

     - Add doc for a new requiresCommunities flag (shown only in Communities
     mode).
     - interCommunityTravelPct — Public policy, requiresCommunities, %, per-arm,
     default 8,
     0–100. "Chance per second a person travels to another community."
     - tripDuration — Personal control, requiresCommunities, temporal, sim-sec,
     per-arm,
     default 3, min 0.5 max 20. "How long a traveler stays before returning
     home."

     Agent (src/agent.js)

     - Add communityId = -1 (home), awayCommunity = -1 (current cell when away),
     travelState = 'home' ('home' | 'away'), tripTimer = 0.

     UI (src/ui.js, index.html, src/app.js)

     - index.html: add <option value="communities">Communities</option> to
     #populationMode.
     - ui.buildPanel: gate requiresCommunities params on opts.communitiesMode
     (mirrors the
     requiresHouseholds/opts.householdMode pattern).
     - app.js: pass { householdMode, communitiesMode } into buildPanel (boot +
     rebuildPanels).
     The population-mode change handler already rebuilds global params + panels
     + resets;
     extend it to compute both flags. Travel/trip params are read live (no
     special routing).

     Engine (src/simulation.js)

     - reset(): when params.populationMode === 'communities', assign each agent
     a home
     community in contiguous blocks (communityId = floor(i*8/n)) so the seeded
     initial
     infections start in one community; set travelState='home', place inside the
     home
     cell (RNG). (this.households stays null.) Reset does not need to store cell
     rects
     (computed live via helpers).
     - Generalize wanderInZone(a) → wanderInRect(a, rx, ry, rw, rh) (wander +
     reflect off
     the rect walls). Quarantine confinement calls it with the zone; communities
     call it with
     the agent's current cell.
     - _integrate: add a communities branch (before the households branch),
     selected by
     params.populationMode === 'communities':
       - For each agent: if inQuarantine → wanderInRect(zone); continue.
       - home: with prob interCommunityTravelPct/100 * dt, start a trip — pick a
     random other
     community, travelState='away', tripTimer=tripDuration, teleport into the
     target cell.
       - away: tripTimer -= dt; when ≤0 → teleport back into the home cell,
     travelState='home'.
       - Then wanderInRect within the current cell (home or away).
     Social-distancing steer still
     applies via the existing per-agent isDistancer (individual distancing works
     inside a
     cell). No quarantine keepOut needed — community cells exclude the reserved
     ward.
     - Infection + recovery loop is shared/unchanged (proximity based;
     maybeEnterQuarantine
     and "skip quarantined as sources" already mode-agnostic). On recovery in
     communities mode,
     also reset travelState='home', awayCommunity=-1, and teleport into the home
     cell.
     - render: when communities mode, draw the 8 community cell borders (subtle
     rects) as a
     grid; the bottom-right cell is drawn by the existing quarantine renderer
     when enabled, else
     a faint "reserved" outline. Agents render normally (travelers appear in
     their current
     cell). No household connecting lines in this mode.

     Critical files

     - src/params.js — two new params + requiresCommunities flag.
     - src/agent.js — community/travel fields.
     - src/ui.js — requiresCommunities gating.
     - src/app.js — third mode option wiring; pass communitiesMode to panels.
     - src/simulation.js — grid helpers, block assignment, communities movement
     branch,
     wanderInRect, grid rendering, recovery-home handling; quarantineZone() →
     bottom-right cell.
     - index.html — Communities option.

     Verification

     - Syntax: parse-only check of src/*.js via osascript -l JavaScript.
     - Headless engine test (communities, pop 200, initialInfected 7, seed
     fixed):
       - travel 0% → outbreak stays in the seed community: distinct infected
     communities ≈ 1,
     total removed ≈ one community's size (~25), not the whole population.
       - travel high (e.g. 40%) → spreads across communities: removed ≫ 25,
     multiple
     communities infected.
       - Confinement: every non-quarantined agent lies within its current cell's
     rect
     (home or away) across sampled frames; none in the reserved bottom-right
     cell.
       - Trip duration: average time in the away state ≈ tripDuration.
       - Same-seed determinism holds.
     - Manual/browser: open index.html, pick Communities → a 3×3 grid appears
     with 8
     populated cells and a reserved bottom-right; raising Inter-community travel
     visibly sends
     dots hopping between cells and lets the epidemic jump communities; enabling
     quarantine
     turns the bottom-right cell into the ward.
     (No browser here — no Node — so this step is for the user.)

     Out of scope

     - Tunable community count (fixed at 8 per the 3×3 layout).
     - Households nested within communities.
     - Walking (vs teleporting) between communities.

❯ how is the inter community travel global % implemented?                       

⏺ Quick correction first: it's actually a per-arm parameter (under Public
  policy), not global — each arm has its own value, so you can A/B "travel
  restricted vs not." It's shared across the whole population within an arm,
  which may be what you meant by "global."

  How it's implemented — as a per-second probability (a hazard rate), evaluated
  every simulation substep in the communities branch of _integrate
  (src/simulation.js):

  var travelRate = (this.params.interCommunityTravelPct || 0) / 100; // e.g. 8% 
  → 0.08 per sim-second
  ...
  if (a.travelState === 'home') {
    if (travelRate > 0 && rng.next() < travelRate * dt) {   // <-- the roll
      var tgt = rng.int(0, 6);                 // random OTHER community
      if (tgt >= a.communityId) { tgt++; }     // 0..7 excluding home
      a.awayCommunity = tgt; a.travelState = 'away'; a.tripTimer = tripDuration;
      // teleport into the target cell
    }
  }

  Key properties:

  - Per-second probability. The slider value ÷ 100 is a rate per sim-second.
  Each substep of duration dt (sim-seconds), a home person starts a trip with
  probability travelRate × dt. So "8%" ≈ an 8% chance per sim-second of leaving
  on a trip. (Formally a Poisson process: chance of having traveled within T
  seconds ≈ 1 − e^(−rate·T).)
  - Only home people roll. Someone already away doesn't re-roll — they just
  count down tripTimer and return. So the rate governs trip starts, and trip
  length is the separate Trip-duration knob.
  - Scales with the time multiplier. Because dt is sim-time (the global time
  multiplier is already baked into it), speeding up time produces proportionally
  more trips per real second — consistent with every other temporal quantity.
  - Deterministic. The roll uses the per-step RNG seeded from (seed, sim-time),
  so both arms behave identically when the value matches, and diverge only where
  it differs.
  - Destination. A uniformly random other community (never the home cell, never
  the reserved quarantine cell), reached by an instant teleport.

  One consequence you saw in testing: 8%/sec is actually quite high — over a
  ~12s infectious period there's a large cumulative chance each person travels,
  so the disease reaches all communities. If you want travel to be a rarer
  event, use a much smaller value (e.g. 1–2%).

  Want me to change the semantics (e.g. make it a lower-range slider, or
  reinterpret it as "% of people who ever travel" rather than a per-second
  rate)?

  until it arrives. Return trips animate the same way.

          +D;

          +ur; }

⏺ Transit works: at travel 0% nobody moves between cells (contained to 25 in one
  community); at 4%/40% people are seen in the traveling state and the epidemic
  spreads across all 8 communities. Determinism holds.

  What changed:
  - src/agent.js — added a traveling state plus in-transit destination fields
  (travelTX/TY, travelToHome).
  - src/simulation.js —
    - When a trip starts, the person enters traveling and moves in a straight 
  line across the map toward a random point in the destination cell at
  TRAVEL_SPEED (4× normal), then settles into that cell for the trip; the return
  trip animates the same way back home.
    - While in transit they're "on the road" — curCell returns -2, so they
  neither infect nor get infected until they arrive, and walls don't apply
  during the hop.

  So now you'll see dots physically travel between communities instead of
  blinking in.

⏺ User approved Claude's plan
  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/inherited-pondering-ripple.md · /plan to 
     edit
     Plan: Batch experiment mode (headless A/B across all population modes)

     Context

     Add a third view mode, Batch, alongside Single and Compare. It has no 
     simulation
     canvases — just a Control and a Test parameter panel (restricted to the
     controls common
     to all population modes) and a Run button. Running it executes 20 paired 
     headless
     iterations per population mode (Individuals, Households, Communities) and
     reports, in
     three tables (one per mode), the delta, p-value, and significance for two
     outcomes:
     peak infected and total removed. This turns the tool from a visual explorer
     into a
     lightweight experiment harness for "does this intervention actually help,
     and is it
     statistically significant across population structures?"

     Confirmed decisions

     - Metrics: two rows per table — Peak infected and Total removed.
     - Paired design (shared seed): iteration i runs Control and Test from the
     same seed
     (differ only by params); compute per-iteration delta = test − control;
     paired t-test
     over the 20 deltas. Significant if p < 0.05.
     - Scope: Control vs Test differ only by the common per-arm controls
     (disease,
     social distancing, hygiene, quarantine). Mode-specific params (household
     size, inter-
     community travel, trip duration) use their defaults. Population &
     initially-infected are
     shared across both arms (batch-wide).

     Design

     View mode (index.html, styles.css, src/app.js)

     - Add a Batch button to the view toggle (Single | Compare | Batch).
     - app.js: extend viewMode to include 'batch'. In setView('batch', …):
       - Add class batch-view to #arms; CSS hides every .arm-sim and .arm-chart,
     shows
     both arms' .arm-params panels side by side, and reveals a new batch results
     section.
       - Rebuild both panels with { householdMode:false, communitiesMode:false }
     so only the
     common params show (mode-specific ones stay hidden). Quarantine knobs still
     gate on
     each arm's quarantineEnabled.
       - The rAF loop skips stepping/rendering when viewMode==='batch' (nothing
     to draw).
       - Disable the Balance button state as today (enabled in batch too —
     copying is useful).
     - Add a batch control strip: Run experiment button, an iterations input
     (default
     20), and a progress indicator; plus a #batchResults container for the three
     tables.

     Stats (src/stats.js, new)

     - pairedTTest(deltas) → { meanDelta, p, n }:
       - mean, sample sd (n−1), se = sd/√n, t = mean/se, df = n−1.
       - Two-tailed p via the regularized incomplete beta: p = I_x(df/2, 1/2)
     with
     x = df/(df + t²). Implement betai/betacf + gammaln (Numerical-Recipes
     style).
       - Degenerate cases: sd === 0 → p = (mean === 0) ? 1 : 0.

     Batch runner (src/batch.js, new)

     - runHeadless(armParams, seed, mode):
       - Clone armParams, set populationMode = mode, build a new App.Simulation(
     { width: BATCH_W, height: BATCH_H, params, seed }) (fixed canonical size,
     e.g. 360²).
       - Step with a fixed dt (e.g. step(0.5, 1)) until counts().i === 0 or a
     sim-time cap (e.g. 400 s) — early-stop makes typical runs short.
       - Return { peak: sim.peakInfected, removed: sim.counts().r }.
     - runBatch(controlParams, testParams, iterations, onProgress, onDone):
       - For each mode in ['individuals','households','communities'], for i in
     0..N-1:
     seed = BASE + i; run control and test at that seed; record
     deltaPeak, deltaRemoved, and the raw means.
       - Async + chunked: process one (mode, iteration) task per setTimeout(…,0)
     tick,
     calling onProgress so the UI stays responsive and shows progress (120 runs
     total).
       - After each mode, compute pairedTTest for peak and removed → table rows.
     - Reuses the existing headless-capable Simulation (step needs no canvas; it
     already
     tracks peakInfected and counts()), so no engine changes.

     Results tables (src/batch.js render)

     - One table per mode with columns: Metric · Control mean · Test mean · 
     Delta · p-value ·
     Significant? and rows Peak infected, Total removed. Significant cells
     styled
     (e.g. green/red). Delta = test − control (negative = intervention reduced
     the outcome).

     Critical files

     - index.html — Batch toggle button + batch control strip + #batchResults.
     - styles.css — batch-view layout (hide sim/chart, show panels + tables),
     table styling.
     - src/app.js — 'batch' view mode, panel rebuild with common-only opts, loop
     skip, wire
     Run button to App.Batch.runBatch.
     - src/stats.js (new) — paired t-test + incomplete beta.
     - src/batch.js (new) — headless runner, async orchestration, table
     rendering.
     - Load order in index.html: add stats.js and batch.js before app.js.

     Verification

     - Syntax: parse-only check of src/*.js via osascript -l JavaScript.
     - Stats unit test (JavaScriptCore): pairedTTest against a known dataset
     (compare
     t and p to a reference value); check degenerate cases (all-zero deltas → p
     = 1).
     - Batch behaviour test (headless, small N for speed): for one mode,
     Control social-distancing 0% vs Test 80% → mean delta on Total removed is
     negative and
     p small/significant; Control == Test params → deltas ≈ 0 and not 
     significant.
     Confirm all three modes produce finite means and the 3×2 result cells
     populate.
     - Manual/browser: open index.html, click Batch → canvases disappear,
     Control &
     Test panels show only common controls; set a difference, Run → progress
     advances and three
     tables fill with delta/p/significance. (No browser here — no Node — so this
     is for the user.)

     Notes / trade-offs

     - 20 × 3 × 2 = 120 headless runs; each runs to epidemic end. Executed async
     in chunks
     with a progress bar so the page stays responsive; at large Population it
     may take some
     seconds — lowering Population speeds it up. (A spatial-grid neighbor
     optimization is a
     possible future speed-up but is out of scope here.)

     Out of scope

     - Web-worker parallelism (kept on the main thread, chunked, to stay
     file://-safe).
     - Configurable per-mode-specific params in batch (they use defaults).

⏺ Everything checks out:

  Stats — paired t-test verified against a hand-computed dataset (mean 2.5,
  t≈7.64, p≈0.0001), and the degenerate all-zero case returns p=1.

  Batch behavior (Control social-distancing 0% vs Test 80%, per mode):

  ┌─────────────┬─────────────────────────┬────────┬─────────┬─────────────┐
  │    Mode     │ Removed: control → test │ Delta  │    p    │ Significant │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Individuals │ 150 → 149.3             │ −0.7   │ 0.102   │ No          │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Households  │ 150 → 19.0              │ −131.0 │ <0.0001 │ Yes         │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Communities │ 150 → 149.3             │ −0.7   │ 0.102   │ No          │
  └─────────────┴─────────────────────────┴────────┴─────────┴─────────────┘

  Identical params → delta 0, p=1, not significant. The results are sensible:
  household-level distancing is dramatically effective (matches the earlier
  model), while individual distancing barely dents a highly transmissible
  disease — and it's now quantified with significance across population
  structures.

  What was built:
  - src/stats.js (new) — paired two-tailed t-test with incomplete-beta p-value.
  - src/batch.js (new) — headless runner: for each mode, N paired iterations
  (shared seed → paired), reuses the existing Simulation (no engine change),
  runs async in chunks with progress, computes stats, and renders the three
  tables.
  - index.html — a Batch view button, a batch control strip (Run button,
  iterations input default 20, progress), a #batchResults container, and loads
  stats.js/batch.js.
  - src/app.js — third 'batch' view mode: hides canvases, shows Control + Test
  panels with common-only params, skips the sim loop, and wires the Run button.
  Diff highlighting works in batch too.
  - styles.css — batch layout (two panels side by side, hidden sim/chart) and
  result-table styling.

  All 9 JS files parse. I couldn't do a visual browser pass (no Node here), so
  that's for you to eyeball. Note: the default population is 300, so 120
  headless runs may take a handful of seconds — it runs async with a progress
  readout so the page stays responsive; lowering Population speeds it up.

❯ I want to make a few UI changes. The top bar, where you have the global       
  variables and the ability to start and stop an experiment, should be laid out 
  differently.                                                                  
  The layout should have two rows:                                              
  1. Global Parameters (or something similar): It should have the.speed, time   
  multiplier, population, and initially infected fields, along with shared      
  seed.                                                                         
  2. Test Mode: It should have the selector Single Compare or Patch. Right next 
  to it should be the experiment start and reset controls. Maybe I have a       
  background for both of those buttons, so pause could be in gray, play could   
  be green, etc., and reset could be in a different color.                      
  There is a balance arms field, and I want to move it as a link icon between   
  the control arm and the treatment arm. You can have it turned on, and then    
  whenever it's turned on, both the controls sync. When you click it again, it  
  turns off, so you have the ability to modify the parameters for a certain arm 
  by itself.                                                                    
  Another thing that I would want to add is a tooltip or an information icon    
  right next to every single control. It should have help show up when you      
  hover on top of it. The help should have a short description of what this     
  parameter means and what it means in real-life terms. For example, the        
  infection radius could tell how communicable that disease is. It should also  
  have what would happen if you increase it or decrease it, like what the       
  real-world indicators are that could impact this parameter. For example,      
  social distancing could be how many people are social distancing, and hygiene 
  and prevention could be whether people are taking the right precautions to    
  make sure that the disease doesn't spread, like handwashing, no kissing, no   
  hugging, maintaining good hygiene standards, etc., and what would happen if   
  you increase or decrease it.

      11 +  function infoIcon(help) {

      15 +    var tip = document.createElement('div');

      17 +    function line(cls, boldText, text) {                       
      18 +      var el = document.createElement('div');                  
      19 +      el.className = cls;                                      
      20 +      if (boldText) { var b = document.createElement('b'); b.te
         +xtContent = boldText; el.appendChild(b); }                     
      21 +      el.appendChild(document.createTextNode(text));           
      22 +      tip.appendChild(el);                                     
      23 +    }

      25 +      line('tip-desc', '', help);

         +; }                                                            
      30 +    }                                                          
      31 +    span.appendChild(tip);

⏺ All three UI changes are implemented and statically verified (all files parse,
  every element id matches, engine unaffected). Here's what changed:

  1. Two-row top bar (index.html, styles.css)
  - Row 1 — Global parameters: dot speed, time multiplier, population mode,
  population, initially-infected, shared seed.
  - Row 2 — Experiment: the Single/Compare/Batch selector, then the run
  controls. Play is green, Pause is gray, Reset is amber
  (btn-play/btn-pause/btn-reset, toggled in app.js).

  2. Balance → a link toggle between the arms (index.html, app.js, styles.css)
  - Removed the old top-bar "Balance arms" button; added a 🔗 toggle centered 
  between the Control and Test arms (vertical divider in Compare, between the
  two panels in Batch; hidden in Single).
  - Default OFF (arms independent). Clicking it copies Control→Test and turns on
  live 2-way sync — editing either arm mirrors to the other (values, quarantine
  toggle, and behavior). Click again to unlink and edit each arm alone. Active
  state is highlighted.

  3. Info icon + rich tooltip on every control (params.js, ui.js, styles.css)
  - Each control now has an ⓘ icon; hovering shows a card with what it means 
  (with a real-world analogy) plus Increase: / Decrease: lines describing the

⏺ Fixed and verified. Here's the situation and what changed:

  What it was doing:
  - Within a batch — iterations already varied: iteration i used a distinct
  seed, so the 20 layouts (dot positions, and therefore who gets impacted and
  the peak/total) differed across iterations. Control vs Test within an
  iteration shared the seed (paired, by design).
  - Re-running the whole batch — used a fixed base seed, so every click of "Run
  experiment" reproduced the identical 20 seeds and identical tables. That's the
  part that wasn't truly randomized.

  The fix (src/batch.js): each runBatch call now picks a fresh random base seed
  (runBase = random), and iteration i uses runBase + i. So:
  - Iterations within a run each start from a different randomized state ✅
  - Control and Test stay paired (same seed each iteration) ✅
  - Re-running the batch now gives a new randomized set of results ✅

  Verified headlessly: two consecutive batch runs produced different means and
  p-values (e.g., control-removed 150.00 vs 149.88, p 0.094 vs 0.275),
  confirming genuine run-to-run randomization.

  Note there are now uncommitted changes stacked up: the top-bar/link/tooltip UI
  work plus this batch fix. You said "that's the first one," so I'll hold on
  committing until you're ready — what's the next item?

❯ One of the features that I would want to add is the ability to upload an      
  Anthropic Claude key and help the user analyze the results of an experiment.  
  If we are in the single-experiment mode, we should send the parameters for    
  the experiment. What do those parameters mean? What values were they set at,  
  and how did the time series look? For example, why did we see the infection   
  peak, and what was the outcome for the overall population as opposed to the   
  removed people or the susceptible people?                                     
  The response should be succinct and not overly verbose, and it should explain 
  to the user in simple terms what happened.                                    
  If you're in the compare mode, then all of this information should be synced  
  for both the test and the control arms. We would want to analyze the results  
  and see a couple of things:                                                   
  1. Were the outcomes inline with what we had expected? For example, if        
  quarantine was enabled for the treatment arm but in the control arm           
  quarantine was disabled, and if the peak infection rate or the susceptible    
  population remained high and the peak infection rate remained low, this would 
  mean that the treatment is working as expected. If there were any surprises,  
  or if there were no changes that were seen despite quarantine controls being  
  enabled in the treatment arm, we should look at what we can change to make    
  sure we get a reading that is representative of the change that we have made. 
  We can change the population and the initially infected values as well.       
  2. When we are in the batch mode, we should also do something similar after   
  the results are ready for the three population modes. We should be able to    
  tell the user what exactly happened. If it was an expected result, we should  
  acknowledge that. If it was not an expected result, what would be changed to  
  make sure that we do get a statistically significant reading?                 
  I want this capability to be enabled across all experiment types and all      
  population modes, and the ability to add an Anthropic key should be made      
  optional. Let me know if you have any clarifying product questions.
